## Time Management Methodology

- [12 Week Year System](https://12weekyear.com/) - Time management methodology that replaces annual planning with 12-week cycles, creating urgency and focus by treating each quarter as a complete year for goal-setting and execution. ([Read more](/details/12-week-year-system.md)) `Goal Setting` `Quarterly Planning` `Execution`
- [Atomic Habits Principles](https://jamesclear.com/atomic-habits) - James Clear's system for building good habits and breaking bad ones through tiny changes, featuring time management strategies like implementation intentions, habit stacking, and the two-minute rule. ([Read more](/details/atomic-habits-principles.md)) `Habit Formation` `James Clear` `Behavior Change`
- [Circadian Rhythm-Based Scheduling](https://www.asianefficiency.com/productivity/ultradian-rhythms/) - Time management approach that aligns work schedule with natural biological rhythms, scheduling demanding tasks during peak alertness and routine tasks during energy dips. ([Read more](/details/circadian-rhythm-based-scheduling.md)) `Circadian Rhythm` `Energy Management` `Biological Rhythms`
- [Energy Management Principles](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/4-ways-to-manage-your-energy-and-have-a-balanced-productive-workday) - Modern productivity approach that focuses on managing physical, mental, and emotional energy rather than just time, aligning tasks with natural energy levels for sustainable high performance. ([Read more](/details/energy-management-principles.md)) `Energy Management` `Sustainable Productivity` `Circadian Rhythm`
- [Essentialism](https://gregmckeown.com/books/essentialism/) - Philosophy and methodology by Greg McKeown focused on doing less but better. The disciplined pursuit of less, emphasizing only the vital few activities that truly matter. ([Read more](/details/essentialism.md)) `Philosophy` `Prioritization` `Focus`
- [Fixed Schedule Productivity](https://calnewport.com/my-oldest-productivity-strategy/) - Cal Newport's time management strategy of choosing an ideal work schedule and protecting it rigorously, forcing constraint-driven productivity and work-life balance. ([Read more](/details/fixed-schedule-productivity.md)) `Cal Newport` `Boundaries` `Work Life Balance`
- [Francesco Cirillo's Pomodoro Technique Origins](https://www.pomodorotechnique.com/) - The creation story of the Pomodoro Technique in the 1980s when Francesco Cirillo used a tomato-shaped kitchen timer as a university student, developing the 25-minute work interval system that became one of the world's most popular time management methods. ([Read more](/details/francesco-cirillos-pomodoro-technique-origins.md)) `Pomodoro` `History` `Focus` `Story`
- [Gary Keller's ONE Thing Focusing Question](https://www.the1thing.com/) - The Focusing Question from Gary Keller's 2012 bestseller 'The ONE Thing': 'What's the ONE thing I can do such that by doing it everything else will be easier or unnecessary?' - a prioritization framework for identifying highest-leverage activities. ([Read more](/details/gary-kellers-one-thing-focusing-question.md)) `Prioritization` `Focus` `Leverage` `Essentialism`
- [Getting Things Done](https://gettingthingsdone.com/) - Seminal productivity book by David Allen introducing the GTD method, a comprehensive five-step system for managing tasks and projects with clarity through capture, clarify, organize, reflect, and engage. ([Read more](/details/getting-things-done.md)) `Book` `GTD` `Productivity System`
- [Getting Things Done: The Art of Stress-Free Productivity](https://gettingthingsdone.com) - Foundational productivity book by David Allen introducing the GTD methodology, a comprehensive system for managing tasks and projects with clarity and efficiency. ([Read more](/details/getting-things-done-the-art-of-stress-free-productivity.md)) `GTD` `Productivity System` `Books`
- [Ivy Lee Method](https://jamesclear.com/ivy-lee) - 100-year-old productivity technique involving writing down six most important tasks each evening, prioritizing them, and focusing on one at a time until completion. ([Read more](/details/ivy-lee-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Daily Planning` `Focus`
- [Maker's Schedule, Manager's Schedule](https://www.paulgraham.com/makersschedule.html) - Time management framework by Paul Graham distinguishing between makers who need half-day blocks for creative work and managers who operate in hourly meeting intervals. ([Read more](/details/makers-schedule-managers-schedule.md)) `Work Patterns` `Scheduling` `Deep Work`
- [Most Important Task (MIT)](https://zenhabits.net/purpose-your-day-most-important-task/) - Daily planning method where you identify 1-3 most important tasks each day and complete them before anything else. Ensures critical work gets done regardless of daily chaos. ([Read more](/details/most-important-task-mit.md)) `Prioritization` `Daily Planning` `Focus`
- [The 12 Week Year](https://12weekyear.com) - Goal-setting system by Brian P. Moran that compresses annual goals into 12-week sprints, creating urgency and focus to achieve more in less time. ([Read more](/details/the-12-week-year.md)) `Goal Setting` `Planning` `Books`
- [Ultradian Rhythm Method](https://www.asianefficiency.com/productivity/ultradian-rhythms/) - 90-120 minute natural productivity cycles discovered by Nathaniel Kleiterman, showing 40% higher productivity and 50% less mental fatigue when work aligns with biological energy patterns. ([Read more](/details/ultradian-rhythm-method.md)) `Energy Management` `Neuroscience` `Work Cycles`
- [Warren Buffett's Two-List Strategy](https://jamesclear.com/buffett-focus) - Prioritization method attributed to Warren Buffett. List your top 25 goals, circle the top 5, and actively avoid the remaining 20 until the top 5 are complete. ([Read more](/details/warren-buffetts-two-list-strategy.md)) `Prioritization` `Focus` `Goals`
- [1-3-5 Method](https://blog.workflowy.com/productivity-and-time-management-systems/) - A daily planning productivity system where you commit to accomplishing 1 Major Task, 3 Medium Tasks, and 5 Small Tasks each day, providing a realistic and balanced approach to daily goal-setting that prevents overwhelm while ensuring meaningful progress. ([Read more](/details/one-three-five-method.md)) `Daily Planning` `Prioritization` `Task Management`
- [1-3-5 Rule](https://www.themuse.com/advice/a-better-todo-list-the-135-rule) - A daily prioritization method where you focus on accomplishing one big task, three medium tasks, and five small tasks each day. Created by Alex Cavoulacos, founder of The Muse, this system helps ensure your most important work gets done by recognizing realistic capacity limits. ([Read more](/details/1-3-5-rule.md)) `Prioritization` `Productivity` `Daily Planning`
- [10X Rule](https://www.grantcardone.com/books/the-10x-rule) - A productivity and success methodology by Grant Cardone emphasizing setting targets 10 times higher than initially planned and taking 10 times the action believed necessary to achieve exceptional results. ([Read more](/details/10x-rule.md)) `Goal Setting` `Massive Action` `Productivity`
- [1440 Minutes Rule](https://www.timetrackapp.com/en/blog/1440-rule-of-time-management/) - A time management technique that reframes the 24-hour day as 1440 individual minutes, encouraging minute-by-minute planning and mindful allocation of time by thinking in smaller, more tangible units. ([Read more](/details/1440-minutes-rule.md)) `Time Management` `Mindfulness` `Productivity`
- [15 Secrets Successful People Know About Time Management](https://kevinkruse.com/15-secrets-successful-people-know-about-time-management/) - A New York Times bestseller by Kevin Kruse based on research with billionaires, Olympic athletes, straight-A students, and entrepreneurs that reveals productivity habits including why millionaires don't use to-do lists and how to maximize energy through sleep, diet, exercise, and breaks. ([Read more](/details/15-secrets-successful-people-know-about-time-management.md)) `Productivity` `Research` `Habits` `Book`
- [15-Minute Increment Method](https://www.memtime.com/blog/15-minute-increments-for-productivity) - Time tracking and productivity methodology that divides work hours into 15-minute blocks, creating manageable chunks that enable focused work, reduce task-initiation barriers, and provide frequent micro-wins. Each 8-hour workday becomes 32 measurable units with documented accomplishments. ([Read more](/details/15-minute-increment-method.md)) `Timeboxing` `Micro Productivity` `Focus` `Tracking` `Incremental`
- [168 Hours Method](https://lauravanderkam.com/books/168-hours/) - Time management philosophy by Laura Vanderkam based on viewing time as a weekly resource of 168 hours, encouraging tracking to understand actual time usage and make intentional choices about priorities. ([Read more](/details/168-hours-method.md)) `Time Audit` `Weekly Planning` `Priorities` `Work Life Balance` `Awareness`
- [50-10 Method](https://marginalgains.blog/50-10-focus-method) - Extended Pomodoro-style technique using 50-minute focused work sessions followed by 10-minute breaks, designed to allow deeper flow states while maintaining the productive work-to-rest ratio. ([Read more](/details/50-10-method.md)) `Focus` `Deep Work` `Breaks`
- [52/17 Rule](https://www.insightful.io/blog/52-17-time-tracking) - A productivity method based on research by DeskTime showing that the most productive employees work for 52 minutes followed by 17-minute breaks. This technique optimizes focus and rest cycles for maximum productivity and well-being. ([Read more](/details/5217-rule.md)) `Breaks` `Focus` `Productivity` `Work Rest Cycle`
- [80/20 Rule for Time Management](https://uplyrn.com/post-details/time-management-strategies-students) - Application of the Pareto Principle to time management, recognizing that 80% of results come from 20% of efforts. Helps identify and prioritize high-impact activities while reducing time spent on low-value tasks. ([Read more](/details/8020-rule-for-time-management.md)) `Pareto` `Prioritization` `Efficiency`
- [ABC Analysis Method](https://www.timedoctor.com/blog/abc-method-time-management/) - A time management and prioritization technique introduced by Dr. Alan Lakein that categorizes tasks into A (urgent and important), B (important but can wait), and C (nice-to-have) categories for effective task prioritization. ([Read more](/details/abc-analysis-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Task Management` `Productivity System` `Decision Making`
- [Agile Results](https://www.asianefficiency.com/task-management/6-ways-agile-results-is-better-than-gtd/) - Simplified productivity system focusing on three outcomes for the week and three for the day, emphasizing results over tasks and flexible iteration over rigid planning, particularly effective for users seeking GTD alternatives. ([Read more](/details/agile-results.md)) `Outcomes` `Agile` `Simplicity` `Gtd Alternative`
- [Agile Time Management](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Time management approach adapted from Agile software development principles. Emphasizes sprints, retrospectives, continuous improvement, and adaptive planning rather than rigid long-term schedules. ([Read more](/details/agile-time-management.md)) `Agile` `Sprints` `Continuous Improvement`
- [Alan Lakein - Swiss Cheese Method Creator](https://hubstaff.com/blog/swiss-cheese-method/) - Productivity expert who developed the Swiss Cheese Method in his 1974 book 'How to Get Control of Your Time and Life'. Lakein's approach involves punching holes in daunting tasks by tackling small, manageable pieces that can be completed in 5 minutes or less. ([Read more](/details/alan-lakein-swiss-cheese-method-creator.md)) `Task Initiation` `Procrastination` `Incremental Progress`
- [Alex Cavoulacos - 1-3-5 Rule Creator](https://www.themuse.com/advice/a-better-todo-list-the-135-rule) - Founder and president of The Muse who created the 1-3-5 Rule productivity system. Cavoulacos developed this method to help professionals accomplish one big task, three medium tasks, and five small tasks daily, recognizing realistic capacity limits. ([Read more](/details/alex-cavoulacos-1-3-5-rule-creator.md)) `Prioritization` `Productivity` `Daily Planning` `The Muse`
- [ALPS Method](https://www.ionos.com/startupguide/productivity/time-management-techniques/) - A German time management methodology consisting of simple steps that resembles hiking in the mountains (ALPS stands for German words), focusing on systematic daily planning and realistic time allocation. ([Read more](/details/alps-method.md)) `Planning` `German` `Daily Planning`
- [Andrew Huberman Focus Protocols](https://www.hubermanlab.com) - Neuroscience-based time management protocols developed by Stanford neuroscientist Andrew Huberman, emphasizing 90-minute focus blocks aligned with ultradian rhythms and specific recommendations for optimizing deep work sessions. ([Read more](/details/andrew-huberman-focus-protocols.md)) `Neuroscience` `Deep Work` `90 Minute Blocks`
- [Animedoro Technique](https://www.reddit.com/r/anime/comments/hqbvtz/the_animedoro_technique/) - A productivity technique that combines focused work sessions with anime episode watching as rewards, typically involving 40-60 minutes of work followed by a 20-minute anime break. ([Read more](/details/animedoro-technique.md)) `Productivity` `Focus` `Breaks`
- [Anthony Trollope Method](https://jamesclear.com/anthony-trollope) - Victorian-era writing productivity method involving writing 250 words every 15 minutes with a pocket watch, practiced daily from 5-8 AM by novelist Anthony Trollope who produced 47 novels using this disciplined, time-measured approach that prioritized application over inspiration. ([Read more](/details/anthony-trollope-method.md)) `Writers` `Discipline` `Time Blocking` `Productivity System`
- [Anti-Goals Method](https://gobeflo.com/blogs/news/the-power-of-anti-goals) - An unconventional goal-setting approach that focuses on identifying and avoiding undesirable outcomes rather than pursuing positive goals. Based on inversion thinking, anti-goals help prevent burnout, maintain focus, and reduce decision fatigue by establishing clear boundaries. ([Read more](/details/anti-goals-method.md)) `Goal Setting` `Boundaries` `Inversion`
- [Anti-Time Management](https://www.oliverburkeman.com/books) - Alternative productivity philosophy questioning traditional time management approaches, advocating for accepting human limitations, embracing finitude, and focusing on meaningful work over optimization. ([Read more](/details/anti-time-management.md)) `Philosophy` `Mindfulness` `Work Life Balance`
- [Asynchronous Work Methodology](https://async.twist.com/) - Work approach where communication and collaboration happen without requiring immediate responses, allowing deep work without constant interruptions. Replaces meetings with documented decisions and updates. ([Read more](/details/asynchronous-work-methodology.md)) `Async Work` `Remote Work` `Flexibility`
- [Atomic Habits](https://jamesclear.com/atomic-habits) - Bestselling book by James Clear on building good habits and breaking bad ones through tiny changes. Provides a proven framework for improving productivity and time management by focusing on 1% improvements. ([Read more](/details/atomic-habits.md)) `Habit Formation` `Productivity` `Self Improvement`
- [Autofocus Method](https://brainmanager.io/blog/personality/autofocus-time-management-technique) - Intuitive task management system created by Mark Forster that uses motivation instead of time management skills by working from one long list and doing whatever feels right first. ([Read more](/details/autofocus-method.md)) `Task Management` `Intuition` `Motivation`
- [Autofocus Task System](http://markforster.squarespace.com/autofocus-system/) - A task management methodology by Mark Forster using intuitive task selection from a single list, allowing your subconscious to guide what to work on next based on what stands out. ([Read more](/details/autofocus-task-system.md)) `Task Management` `Intuitive` `Productivity System` `Mark Forster`
- [Autofocus Time Management](http://markforster.squarespace.com/autofocus-system) - Task management system created by Mark Forster where you work through a long list by asking which task you feel most like doing right now, allowing intuition to guide task selection and time allocation. ([Read more](/details/autofocus-time-management.md)) `Task Management` `Intuition` `Flexibility`
- [Basic Rest-Activity Cycle (BRAC)](https://www.asianefficiency.com/productivity/ultradian-rhythms/) - Natural biological rhythm discovered by sleep researcher Nathaniel Kleitman involving 90-120 minute cycles of high-frequency brain activity followed by 20-minute recovery periods, present during both sleep and waking hours, forming the scientific foundation for productivity techniques like ultradian rhythm scheduling. ([Read more](/details/basic-rest-activity-cycle-brac.md)) `Biological Rhythms` `Neuroscience` `Productivity` `Breaks` `Science`
- [Batching Method](https://todoist.com/productivity-methods/task-batching) - Productivity technique of grouping similar tasks together and completing them in one dedicated session. Reduces context switching, improves efficiency, and minimizes mental fatigue. ([Read more](/details/batching-method.md)) `Efficiency` `Productivity` `Batching`
- [Benjamin Franklin Time Blocking Method](https://super-productivity.com/guides/time-boxing-method/) - Historical time management practice where Benjamin Franklin meticulously detailed hour-by-hour activities including work, rest, and chores. Considered an early adoption of structured daily timeboxing. ([Read more](/details/benjamin-franklin-time-blocking-method.md)) `History` `Time Blocking` `Classics`
- [Brad Isaac's Seinfeld Strategy Story](https://jamesclear.com/stop-procrastinating-seinfeld-strategy) - The origin story of Don't Break the Chain method, where comedian Brad Isaac asked Jerry Seinfeld for advice backstage and Seinfeld shared his calendar X-marking system for daily writing practice, though Seinfeld later denied creating the method. ([Read more](/details/brad-isaacs-seinfeld-strategy-story.md)) `Habits` `Consistency` `Motivation` `Story`
- [Brooke Castillo (Monday Hour One Creator)](https://www.thelifecoachschool.com/monday-hour-one) - Life coach and founder of The Life Coach School who developed the Monday Hour One time management system, emphasizing weekly planning through intentional calendar scheduling rather than reactive to-do lists. ([Read more](/details/brooke-castillo-monday-hour-one-creator.md)) `Life Coaching` `Calendar Planning` `Intentionality` `Self Commitment`
- [Building a Second Brain (Time Management Application)](https://www.buildingasecondbrain.com/) - Methodology for capturing and organizing information to free up mental bandwidth for focus and productivity, reducing time spent searching for information and enabling better time allocation to high-value work. ([Read more](/details/building-a-second-brain-time-management-application.md)) `Knowledge Management` `Productivity` `Information Management` `Focus` `Systems`
- [Bullet Journal Method](https://bulletjournal.com/blogs/faq/what-is-the-bullet-journal-method) - Analog productivity system created by Ryder Carroll that combines rapid logging, mindfulness, and intentional task management in a customizable notebook format. Originally developed to manage ADHD, it uses short bulleted entries, migration rituals, and time-based logs (daily, weekly, monthly) to track the past, organize the present, and plan the future. ## Practices

- [Cal Newport Time Blocking](https://www.timeblockplanner.com/) - Deep work time management methodology by Cal Newport involving dividing each day into time blocks with specific tasks assigned to each block. Creates structured schedules that generate massive productivity gains. ([Read more](/details/cal-newport-time-blocking.md)) `Time Blocking` `Deep Work` `Productivity Method`
- [Context Switching Penalty](https://www.apa.org/news/press/releases/2006/03/multitasking) - Cognitive cost incurred when switching between different tasks or projects, including attention residue, ramp-up time, and reduced performance. Research shows switching can cost 20-40% of productive time. ([Read more](/details/context-switching-penalty.md)) `Cognitive Science` `Productivity` `Context Switching`
- [Covey Time Management Matrix](https://www.franklincovey.com/) - Prioritization framework created by Stephen Covey that divides tasks into four quadrants based on urgency and importance. Featured in 'The 7 Habits of Highly Effective People,' it helps identify top-priority tasks for optimized productivity. ([Read more](/details/covey-time-management-matrix.md)) `Methodology` `Prioritization` `Decision Making`
- [Deep Work & Shallow Work Separation](https://calnewport.com/deep-work-rules-for-focused-success-in-a-distracted-world) - Productivity framework by Cal Newport that distinguishes between cognitively demanding deep work and low-value shallow work, advocating for dedicated time blocks and minimization of the latter. ([Read more](/details/deep-work-shallow-work-separation.md)) `Deep Work` `Focus` `Productivity`
- [Focus Time Method](https://www.getclockwise.com/blog/focus-time) - Productivity practice of blocking dedicated time for deep, uninterrupted work on important tasks. ([Read more](/details/focus-time-method.md)) `Focus Time` `Deep Work` `calendar-blocking`
- [Pareto Principle (80/20 Rule)](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-life-hacks/organization/understanding-pareto-principle-of-time-management) - Concept by Vilfredo Pareto stating that 80% of outcomes result from 20% of causes. - [Time Management Matrix](https://www.franklincovey.com/the-7-habits/habit-3/) - Framework for categorizing tasks based on urgency and importance, popularized by Stephen Covey. ## Time Management Principle

- [Leverage in Time Management](https://www.effectiveengineer.com/blog/leverage) - Principle of maximizing output per unit of time by focusing on high-impact activities, delegating, automating, and avoiding low-value work. ([Read more](/details/leverage-in-time-management.md)) `Leverage` `High Impact` `Strategic Thinking`
- [Monotasking Principle](https://www.todoist.com/inspiration/monotasking) - Productivity approach that involves focusing on one task at a time rather than multitasking, based on research showing that human brains are not designed for effective multitasking. ([Read more](/details/monotasking-principle.md)) `Monotasking` `Focus` `Single Tasking`
- [Pareto Principle (80/20 Rule) for Time Management](https://www.investopedia.com/terms/1/80-20-rule.asp) - Time management principle stating that 80% of results come from 20% of efforts. Guides focus toward high-impact activities by identifying which tasks, projects, or activities generate disproportionate value relative to time invested. ([Read more](/details/pareto-principle-8020-rule-for-time-management.md)) `Prioritization` `Efficiency` `Focus`
- [10/90 Planning Rule](https://medium.com/@productivitybee/the-10-90-rule-how-to-save-90-of-your-time-and-get-more-productivity-adcd6ffd9675) - A time management principle stating that spending the first 10% of your time planning and organizing work before starting can save up to 90% of execution time, emphasizing that 10 minutes of planning can save up to 2 hours of wasted effort throughout the day. ([Read more](/details/1090-planning-rule.md)) `Planning` `Efficiency` `Productivity` `Time Management`
- [10/90 Rule of Time Management](https://www.briantracy.com) - Productivity principle from Brian Tracy stating that the first 10% of time spent planning and organizing work will save 90% of the time in execution, emphasizing the importance of preparation over rushing into tasks. ([Read more](/details/1090-rule-of-time-management.md)) `Planning` `Efficiency` `Preparation` `Brian Tracy`
- [80/20 Calendar Rule](https://reclaim.ai/blog/time-blocking-guide) - Time management guideline suggesting never scheduling more than 80% of your available work hours, leaving 20% for unexpected tasks, meeting overruns, breaks, and flexibility to handle the unpredictable. ([Read more](/details/8020-calendar-rule.md)) `Scheduling` `Buffer Time` `Flexibility` `Realistic Planning`
- [90/10 Outcomes Rule](https://www.larksuite.com/en_us/topics/productivity-glossary/90-10-principle) - A productivity principle stating that 10% of your actions account for 90% of your outcomes, related to the Pareto Principle but specifically applied to daily activity selection, suggesting strategic focus on the highest-impact 10% of possible tasks. ([Read more](/details/9010-outcomes-rule.md)) `Pareto` `Prioritization` `Effectiveness` `Strategic Thinking`
- [90/10 Productivity Rule](https://www.hellobonsai.com/blog/time-and-resource-management) - Time management principle stating that 10% of activities produce 90% of outcomes. This rule, also known as the Pareto Principle applied to time, encourages identifying and prioritizing high-impact tasks while eliminating or delegating low-value work. ([Read more](/details/9010-productivity-rule.md)) `Pareto` `Prioritization` `High Impact` `Effectiveness`
- [Delegate or Die Principle](https://www.entrepreneur.com/leadership/delegate-or-die/197802) - Time management philosophy emphasizing that success requires delegating tasks to others, as attempting to do everything personally limits growth and leads to burnout. ([Read more](/details/delegate-or-die-principle.md)) `Delegation` `Leadership` `Scaling`
- [Hofstadter's Law](https://www.researchmasterminds.com/blog/parkinsons-and-hofstadters-laws-time-management) - Cognitive principle stating that tasks always take longer than expected, even when accounting for Hofstadter's Law itself. Proposed by Douglas Hofstadter in 1979, this recursive law helps explain chronic underestimation in project planning. ([Read more](/details/hofstadters-law.md)) `Project Planning` `Estimation` `Cognitive Bias`
- [Jeremy Wright (Pickle Jar Theory Originator)](https://clockify.me/blog/managing-time/pickle-jar-theory/) - Author who coined the Pickle Jar Theory in 2002, using the metaphor of fitting rocks, pebbles, sand, and water into a jar to illustrate the importance of prioritizing critical tasks before filling time with less important activities. ([Read more](/details/jeremy-wright-pickle-jar-theory-originator.md)) `Inventor` `Metaphor` `Prioritization` `Visualization`
- [Next Action Principle](https://gettingthingsdone.com/) - GTD concept that every project must have a clearly defined next physical action. Eliminates ambiguity, reduces procrastination, and makes projects actionable by identifying the very next concrete step. ([Read more](/details/next-action-principle.md)) `GTD` `Action` `Productivity`
- [One-Touch Rule](https://www.psychologytoday.com/us/blog/click-here-for-happiness/201902/the-one-touch-rule) - A productivity principle stating that when you pick up a task or item, handle it completely rather than putting it down to deal with later, reducing clutter and mental load. ([Read more](/details/one-touch-rule.md)) `Productivity Principle` `Decision Making` `Efficiency` `Task Completion`
- [Parkinson's Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkinson's_law) - Observation coined by Cyril Northcote Parkinson in 1955 stating that work expands to fill the time allotted for its completion. Used in time management to set tighter deadlines and avoid unnecessary complexity. ([Read more](/details/parkinsons-law.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Deadlines`
- [Parkinson's Law (Time Management)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkinson%27s_law) - Adage that work expands to fill the time available for its completion, suggesting that setting tighter deadlines and shorter time boxes can actually increase productivity by preventing unnecessary expansion. ([Read more](/details/parkinsons-law-time-management.md)) `Parkinsons Law` `Deadlines` `Timeboxing` `Psychology`
- [Parkinson's Law of Time Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkinson%27s_law) - The principle that work expands to fill the time available for its completion, suggesting that setting tighter deadlines and constraints can increase efficiency and reduce procrastination. ([Read more](/details/parkinsons-law-of-time-management.md)) `Productivity` `Efficiency` `Deadlines` `Constraints` `Psychology`
- [Sam Carpenter (Biological Prime Time Originator)](https://www.larksuite.com/en_us/topics/productivity-glossary/biological-prime-time) - Business systems expert and author who coined the term "biological prime time" in his book Work the System, introducing the concept of identifying and leveraging personal peak productivity hours. ([Read more](/details/sam-carpenter-biological-prime-time-originator.md)) `Inventor` `Author` `Systems Thinking` `Energy Management`
- [The Focusing Question](https://www.the1thing.com/) - Core principle from The ONE Thing book: 'What's the ONE Thing I can do such that by doing it everything else will be easier or unnecessary?' This question helps identify the single most impactful action in any area, creating sequential success through focused effort. ([Read more](/details/the-focusing-question.md)) `Focus` `Prioritization` `Decision Making`
- [Worst First Principle (Eat That Frog)](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/the-truth-about-frogs/) - Brian Tracy's time management principle of tackling most difficult, unpleasant, or important task first thing each day. Based on Mark Twain quote about eating a frog. Ensures priority work gets done regardless of day's chaos, builds momentum, and provides psychological relief. ([Read more](/details/worst-first-principle-eat-that-frog.md)) `Prioritization` `Morning` `Discipline` `Brian Tracy`

## Time Management Technique

- [Eisenhower Decision Matrix](https://www.eisenhower.me/eisenhower-matrix) - Time management framework for prioritizing tasks based on urgency and importance, attributed to President Dwight D. Eisenhower, organizing work into four quadrants for better decision-making. ([Read more](/details/eisenhower-decision-matrix.md)) `Eisenhower Matrix` `Prioritization` `Decision Making`
- [Time Blocking Method](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Productivity technique of scheduling specific blocks of time for different tasks and activities in advance, making deliberate choices about how to spend time rather than reacting to demands as they arise. ([Read more](/details/time-blocking-method.md)) `Time Blocking` `Calendar` `Planning`
- [Timeboxing Technique](https://hbr.org/2018/12/how-timeboxing-works-and-why-it-will-make-you-more-productive) - Science-backed time management strategy that involves allocating fixed, predetermined amounts of time to tasks and sticking to those limits, ranked as the most useful productivity hack in surveys. ([Read more](/details/timeboxing-technique.md)) `Time Blocking` `Scheduling` `Productivity`
- [1-3-5 Technique](https://myhours.com/articles/time-management-skills-techniques-strategies-list) - A daily time management method that structures your to-do list into 1 big task, 3 medium tasks, and 5 small tasks. It provides a simple, focused framework for prioritizing daily work and managing workload capacity. ([Read more](/details/1-3-5-technique.md)) `Task Prioritization` `Daily Planning` `Workload Management`
- [10 Minute Task](https://www.lifehack.org/articles/featured/10-minute-task-technique.html) - A time management technique where every task on your to-do list should be broken down so that it takes no longer than 10 minutes to complete. If a task would take longer, it must be subdivided into smaller, more manageable pieces. This approach prevents procrastination by making every task feel achievable. ([Read more](/details/10-minute-task.md)) `Micro Tasks` `Task Breakdown` `Procrastination`
- [10 Minutes Technique](https://www.inc.com/larry-kim/10-minute-rule.html) - A task-start strategy where you commit to just 10 minutes of effort on a selected task, after which you can stop if you want. The psychological trick is that once you begin, you usually won't want to quit, overcoming initial resistance and procrastination. ([Read more](/details/10-minutes-technique.md)) `Task Start` `Procrastination` `Commitment`
- [112/26 Rule](https://medium.com/illumination/the-pomodoro-technique-is-outdated-use-the-52-17-or-112-26-rules-instead-b00b5143c645) - An extended productivity technique similar to the 52/17 rule, where you work for 112 minutes followed by a 26-minute break. This longer interval is suggested for tasks requiring sustained deep focus and complex problem-solving. ([Read more](/details/11226-rule.md)) `Deep Work` `Breaks` `Focus`
- [5 P's of Time Management](https://timeflip.io/blog/guide-to-popular-time-management-techniques) - A time management framework developed by Henry Mintzberg that emphasizes preparation through five steps: Prioritize, Plan, Prepare, Pace, and Persist. It provides a structured approach for individuals managing multiple projects and responsibilities. ([Read more](/details/5-ps-of-time-management.md)) `Prioritization` `Structured Planning` `Productivity Framework`
- [5/25 Reverse Pomodoro Method](https://www.tiimoapp.com/resource-hub/reverse-pomodoro-technique) - Alternative Pomodoro approach where you work for 5 minutes followed by a 25-minute break, designed to overcome procrastination and task initiation challenges by making starting feel easy. ([Read more](/details/525-reverse-pomodoro-method.md)) `Reverse Pomodoro` `Procrastination` `Adhd`
- [60-30-10 Rule](https://timeanalyticssoftware.com/60-30-10-rule-of-time-management/) - A time management principle suggesting that 60% of your time should be spent on scheduled tasks, 30% on unplanned interruptions and reactive work, and 10% on spontaneous activities or buffer time. ([Read more](/details/60-30-10-rule.md)) `Planning` `Scheduling` `Buffer Time`
- [60-60-30 Technique](https://www.gauraw.com/60-60-30-time-management-productivity-technique/) - A productivity technique that involves working for 60 minutes twice with focused attention, followed by a complete 30-minute break. Often broken down into 50-minute work sessions with 10-minute micro-breaks, creating sustainable work rhythms. ([Read more](/details/60-60-30-technique.md)) `Pomodoro` `Breaks` `Focus`
- [60-Second Brain Dump](https://medium.com/methodical-madness/60-seconds-will-change-your-life-2a131b4f3d2a) - A rapid mental clearing technique where you set a 60-second timer and write down all tasks and thoughts that come to mind, helping reduce mental clutter and identify priorities quickly throughout the day. ([Read more](/details/60-second-brain-dump.md)) `Quick Action` `Mental Clarity` `Planning`
- [60-Second Procrastination Rule](https://eightify.app/summary/productivity-and-time-management/unlocking-the-power-of-the-60-second-procrastination-rule) - A technique that helps bridge the gap between procrastination and action by acknowledging stress and making a behavioral change within 60 seconds, encouraging immediate engagement with tasks. ([Read more](/details/60-second-procrastination-rule.md)) `Procrastination` `Quick Action` `Behavior Change`
- [7 Minute Life](https://www.7minutelife.com/) - A daily time management system that dedicates 7 minutes in the morning to plan your day and 7 minutes in the evening to review accomplishments and prepare tomorrow's plan. ([Read more](/details/7-minute-life.md)) `Daily Planning` `Morning Routine` `Evening Review`
- [7-8-9 Rule](https://www.prioritymanagement.com.au/7-8-9-time-management-rule/) - A time management rule suggesting you wake by 7 AM, start work by 8 AM, and tackle your high-priority tasks by 9 AM, ensuring you complete your most important work when your energy levels are highest. ([Read more](/details/7-8-9-rule.md)) `Morning Routine` `Productivity` `Energy Management`
- [90/20 Rule](https://medium.com/@drpatriciademello80/the-power-of-the-90-20-rule-a-paradigm-shift-in-productivity-aaf5db589c5b) - A productivity technique involving 90% focused effort on a task for a designated period, followed by a 20% break for rejuvenation, representing a paradigm shift from constant work to sustainable, cyclical productivity. ([Read more](/details/9020-rule.md)) `Breaks` `Focus` `Recovery` `Work Rest Cycle`
- [ABC Task System](https://www.mindtools.com/ajg8k4e/the-abc-method-of-time-management) - A hierarchical task classification method that categorizes tasks into three levels of urgency: 'A' for must-do today, 'B' for should-do today, and 'C' for optional tasks. This system helps workers quickly triage their workload and focus on what matters most. ([Read more](/details/abc-task-system.md)) `Task Prioritization` `Daily Planning`
- [ABCDE Method](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/the-abcde-list-technique-for-setting-priorities/) - Priority-setting technique by Brian Tracy categorizing tasks into five levels (A-E) based on importance and consequences, focusing effort on must-do tasks first. ([Read more](/details/abcde-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Task Management` `Productivity`
- [ABCDE Method (Brian Tracy)](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/the-truth-about-frogs/) - Task prioritization technique from Brian Tracy's Eat That Frog system that categorizes tasks into five levels—A (must do), B (should do), C (nice to do), D (delegate), and E (eliminate)—to focus energy on highest-impact activities. ([Read more](/details/abcde-method-brian-tracy.md)) `Prioritization` `Delegation` `Elimination` `Brian Tracy`
- [ABCDE Prioritization Method](https://www.briantracy.com/blog/time-management/abcde-method) - Brian Tracy's task prioritization technique where tasks are labeled A (must do with serious consequences), B (should do with mild consequences), C (nice to do with no consequences), D (delegate), or E (eliminate). ([Read more](/details/abcde-prioritization-method.md)) `Prioritization` `Task Management` `Eat That Frog`
- [Activity Log Time Tracking Method](https://clockify.me/blog/managing-time/bullet-journal-time-tracker/) - A detailed journaling approach to time tracking where individuals record each activity throughout their day along with the time invested, creating intricate chronicles that help identify workflow patterns and optimize time allocation. ([Read more](/details/activity-log-time-tracking-method.md)) `Journaling` `Time Analysis` `Self Tracking`
- [ALPEN Method Time Planning](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/aplen-time-management-technique) - A German time management technique developed by Professor Lothar J. Seiwert involving five steps: listing tasks (Aufgaben), estimating duration (Länge), scheduling buffer time (Pufferzeit), making priority decisions (Entscheidungen), and reviewing the plan (Nachkontrolle). Takes 10-15 minutes daily to organize activities while significantly reducing stress. ([Read more](/details/alpen-method-time-planning.md)) `Planning Method` `German System` `Daily Organization`
- [Batch Processing Method](https://www.timedoctor.com/blog/time-management-tools-and-techniques/) - Time management technique of grouping similar tasks together and completing them in dedicated time blocks to minimize context switching and increase efficiency. ([Read more](/details/batch-processing-method.md)) `Batching` `Efficiency` `Context Switching`
- [Biological Prime Time Method](https://www.insightful.io/blog/peak-productivity-bio-prime-time) - Productivity strategy that involves tracking your energy and focus levels throughout the day for 2-3 weeks to identify your personal peak performance hours, then scheduling your most important work during these naturally high-energy periods. ([Read more](/details/biological-prime-time-method.md)) `Energy Management` `Productivity` `Self Tracking` `Optimization`
- [Body Doubling for ADHD Productivity](https://www.additudemag.com/body-doubling-adhd-work-productivity/) - Productivity technique where working in presence of another person (virtual or in-person) helps ADHD individuals initiate and sustain focus on tasks. The presence creates accountability and external structure, making it easier to start and maintain work. Increasingly popular through virtual co-working platforms. ([Read more](/details/body-doubling-for-adhd-productivity.md)) `Adhd` `Productivity` `Co Working` `Accountability` `Focus`
- [Buffer Time Strategy](https://www.morgen.so/resources/calendar-management/buffer-time) - A calendar management technique that deliberately schedules empty time slots between meetings and tasks to absorb overruns, handle transitions, prevent back-to-back exhaustion, and create space for unexpected work. ([Read more](/details/buffer-time-strategy.md)) `Calendar Management` `Buffer Time` `Scheduling` `Meetings`
- [Calendar Blocking (Time Blocking)](https://clockwise.com/blog/time-blocking) - A scheduling method of blocking specific time periods on your calendar for designated tasks or types of work, treating focus time with the same importance as meetings. ([Read more](/details/calendar-blocking-time-blocking.md)) `Scheduling` `Calendar Management` `Time Blocking` `Focus Time` `Planning`
- [Calendar Time Audit Method](https://reclaim.ai/blog/calendar-time-audit) - Productivity technique that analyzes calendar data to identify time-wasting activities, meeting overload, and misalignment between priorities and actual time allocation. Calendar audits help reclaim 5-10+ hours weekly by revealing gaps between intended and actual time use. ([Read more](/details/calendar-time-audit-method.md)) `Time Audit` `Calendar` `Productivity` `Analysis`
- [Circadian Rhythm Productivity Optimization](https://www.risescience.com/blog/circadian-rhythm-sales-productivity) - Time management approach based on aligning work with natural biological rhythms. Research shows two natural windows of high alertness (late morning and late afternoon) affect focus, attention, learning, and working memory. Optimizing work schedules around these patterns can significantly boost productivity. ([Read more](/details/circadian-rhythm-productivity-optimization.md)) `Circadian Rhythm` `Energy Management` `Productivity`
- [Closed Lists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-to-do_list) - A constraint-based task management approach that limits an active to-do list to a fixed set of tasks, allowing no new additions until current items are completed. This technique prevents the perpetual growth that makes traditional to-do lists feel unmanageable. ([Read more](/details/closed-lists.md)) `Workload Management` `Task Prioritization`
- [Context Switching Technique](https://www.atlassian.com/blog/productivity/time-management-strategies) - Time management approach that groups tasks by the mental energy they require, scheduling deep creative work in mornings without digital interruptions while batching administrative tasks for afternoons. ([Read more](/details/context-switching-technique.md)) `Batching` `Energy Management` `Context`
- [Context-Based Task Organization](https://gettingthingsdone.com/) - GTD methodology principle of organizing tasks by the context where they can be completed (@home, @computer, @phone, @errands) rather than by project. Improves efficiency by grouping similar action modes together. ([Read more](/details/context-based-task-organization.md)) `GTD` `Organization` `Efficiency`
- [Daily Plan Bar (Bullet Journal)](https://rediscoveranalog.com/analog-time-management-in-a-bullet-journal/) - A popular analog time tracking method for bullet journals that serves as a visual time management tool for time-boxing and activity tracking, where users create a bar graph representing their day and plot meetings, appointments, and activities along a timeline. ([Read more](/details/daily-plan-bar-bullet-journal.md)) `Bullet Journal` `Analog` `Time Blocking`
- [Daily Time Blocking Practice](https://reclaim.ai/blog/time-blocking-guide) - Productivity technique where individuals allocate specific time blocks on their calendar for different tasks and activities each day. Research shows time-blocked workweeks can produce the same output as 60+ hour unstructured weeks in just 40 hours. The practice involves planning the entire day hour-by-hour to maximize focus and minimize decision fatigue. ([Read more](/details/daily-time-blocking-practice.md)) `Calendar Method` `Planning Practice` `Productivity Technique`
- [Dave Lee's System](https://davelee.io/productivity) - A productivity system that combines a weekly chart, daily focus area, and Pomodoro timer. Users choose five important areas for the week, and dedicate one area to each day, ensuring balanced progress across multiple priorities while using focused work intervals for execution. ([Read more](/details/dave-lees-system.md)) `Weekly Planning` `Focus Areas` `Pomodoro Variation`
- [Day Theming](https://www.todoist.com/productivity-methods/time-blocking) - Extreme version of task batching where entire days are dedicated to specific types of work, promoted by Cal Newport for deeper concentration than traditional time blocking. ([Read more](/details/day-theming.md)) `Scheduling` `Deep Work` `Batching`
- [Day Theming Productivity Method](https://trackingtime.co/time-tracking-software/day-theming.html) - Time management technique where entire days are dedicated to specific types of work or themes, representing an extreme form of task batching that reduces context switching by organizing work at the daily rather than hourly level. ([Read more](/details/day-theming-productivity-method.md)) `Batching` `Focus` `Context Switching` `Planning`
- [Do It Now Rule](https://www.productivityist.com/the-three-minute-rule/) - A simple productivity rule stating that if a task takes less than 3 minutes, you should do it immediately without any thinking or planning. This eliminates the overhead of scheduling, tracking, or remembering quick tasks and keeps small items from accumulating. ([Read more](/details/do-it-now-rule.md)) `Quick Tasks` `Immediate Action` `Decision Rule`
- [Don't Break the Chain](https://www.writersstore.com/dont-break-the-chain-jerry-seinfeld/) - Habit-building method attributed to Jerry Seinfeld: mark an X on calendar each day you complete target habit. Motivation comes from not breaking the growing chain of X's, building consistency through visual
